Document 003: 10月FOMC議事要旨：12月利上げ視野、少数ながら50bp議論 ～FRBは2027年のインフレ鈍化シナリオを維持～

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月FOMC議事要旨：12月利上げ視野、少数ながら50bp議論 ～FRBは2027年のインフレ鈍化シナリオを維持～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-19
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

FED  Watching

2026 年 11 月 19 日

1/4 
10 月 FOMC 議事要旨：12 月利上げ視野、少数ながら 50bp 議論 
～FRB は 2027 年のインフレ鈍化シナリオを維持～ 
 経済調査部 主任エコノミスト  桂畑 誠治  
（℡：050-5474-7493）

要旨  
・12 月利上げ再開がメインシナリオ：10 月 27〜28 日開催のFOMC議事要旨が公表された。
同会合では政策金利の据え置きが決定されたものの、参加者のほぼ全員が「今後数カ月間（12 月
15〜16 日会合を含む）での利上げ再開」を基本路線として維持していることが確認された。

・タカ派的テールリスクの浮上：一方で、2〜3 名の参加者がデータ次第で「50bpの大幅利上
げ」を検討する余地に言及しており、市場ではタカ派的なテールリスクが意識される内容となっ
た。

・2027 年のインフレ鈍化見通しは不変：FRBのベースライン（基本見通し）では、インフレは
目先高止まりするものの、関税影響の減退やエネルギー・AI関連の供給制約緩和に伴い、2027
年にかけて鈍化し、時間の経過とともに目標の 2％へ収束していくとの見方は崩れていない。

1．新体制下での情報発信と議事 要旨 の重要性  
新体制移行後、会合直後の声明文は大幅に簡素化され、議長の記者会見も短縮されるなど、直
近の直接的な情報発信は絞り込まれている。その分、後日公表される議事要旨は高い情報量を維
持しており、FRBの本音や委員会内部の議論の分布を推し量る最重要ツールとしての重みが増して
いる。もっとも、公表がFOMC終了から 3 週間後となるため、その間に発表された最新データとの
タイムラグ（情報の遅効性）を考慮して評価する必要がある。

2．10 月会合 の政策 判断 と金融 政策 の基本 スタンス  
10 月会合では、FF レート誘導目標レンジ（3.75％〜4.00％）の据え置きが決定された（投票権
を持つ委員ベースでは 12 名中 10 名が賛成、2 名が反対）。据え置きを支持した委員は、「さらなる
情報の蓄積が、インフレ率を 2％の目標へ適時に戻すための適切な政策スタンスを見極める上で役
立つ」と指摘した。今回の据え置きは利上げサイクルの終了ではなく、累積的な引き締め効果と最
新データを確認するための「一時停止（スキップ）」という位置づけである。

### Page 2

2/4 
今後の政策決定については、「新たに入手されるデータ、変化する見通し、リスクバランスに依存
する」とのデータ次第（データ・ディペンデント）の姿勢を強調した。その上で、基本見通しの下
では「9 月の経済・金利予測（SEP：議長自身は今回も提出せず）とおおむね整合的な形で、今後数
カ月間（12 月 15〜16 日の次回会合など）で追加利上げを行うことが適切となる可能性が高い」と
の見解で、参加者のほぼ全員が一致した。

3．経済・物価・雇用の現状認識と 中長期 の見通し  
委員会全体の共通認識を確認すると、景気については「底堅い家計支出と堅調な設備投資に支え
られ、経済活動は引き続き力強いペースで拡大している」との見方で一致した。 
労働市場については「安定しており、最大雇用に近づいている」と判断され、目先も安定した状
態が続き、雇用の上振れリスクと下振れリスクはおおむね均衡していると評価された。 
インフレに関しては、依然として高止まりしており、物価上昇圧力は経済全体に広範に及んでい
るとの指摘が相次いだ。特に高いエネルギー価格や精製燃料の供給制約が足元の押し上げ要因とな
っており、見通しには上振れリスクが意識されている。一方、名目賃金の伸びは緩やかな水準にと
どまり、2％のインフレ目標とおおむね整合的と判断された。 
中長期のインフレ見通しでは、9 月コア CPI（前月比＋0.3％）の上振れを受けても、スタッフの
インフレ予測は 9 月会合時点からほとんど変更されず、参加者の間でも従来の基本見通しが維持さ
れた。大半の参加者は、インフレ率は目先高止まりするものの、関税引き上げに伴う価格転嫁圧力
の減退や、エネルギー価格・AI 関連投資に伴う供給制約（電力・インフラ需要等）の緩和により、
時間の経過とともに（9 月 SEP で示された 2027 年の鈍化軌道に沿って）2％に向けて低下していく
と予想した。中長期的なインフレ期待も引き続き 2％と整合的であり、これが過度な利上げ回数の
積み増し（ターミナルレートの大幅引き上げ）を抑制する根拠となっている。

4．タカ派サプライズ：「 12 月 50bp 利上げ」への言及と今後の焦点  
今回の議事要旨における最大の注目点（タカ派サプライズ）は、9 月 SEP に沿った追加利上げ（25bp
ずつ）というメインシナリオの裏で、大幅利上げの選択肢が議論されていたことである。「新たに入
手されるインフレデータが上振れれば、次回 12 月会合で 25bp を超える利上げ（50bp など）が検討
に値する」と発言した参加者が 2〜3 名いたことが明らかとなった。 
議事要旨の公表前にあたる11月10日に発表された10月コアCPIは前月比＋0.3％と3カ月連続
で市場予想を上回り、3 カ月年率換算ペースでは目標を大幅に上回る高い伸びとなった。さらに、
10 月会合では据え置きに賛成したウォラーFRB 理事も、10 月 CPI の結果を受けて「50bp の利上げ
を検討すべき」との趣旨の発言を行っており、同理事は議事要旨内の「タカ派の少数意見」に含ま
れていたとみられる。

5．マーケットへのインプリケーション  
現時点では 50bp 利上げは少数意見にとどまるが、FRB が「インフレ再燃リスクに対して後手に回

がある。仮に市場金利の低下などによって金融環境の引き締まりが緩めば、利上げペースの加速に
追い込まれる可能性が高い。
12 月 FOMC に向けては、今後発表される 11 月の雇用統計、CPI、ISM 景気指数の結果次第で、市
場の織り込みが「25bp 利上げ」から「50bp 利上げ」へと急速に傾くリスクがある。債券市場ではタ
ーミナルレートの上振れを意識する形で米 2 年債利回りに上昇圧力がかかりやすく、為替市場でも
ドル高・円安地合いが継続しやすい展開が見込まれる。

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國消費動能維持韌性、房市復甦仍受壓抑，通膨風險與 AI 金融隱憂牽動聯
準會政策走向 
經濟 
⚫ 10 月美國零售銷售月增 0.2%、年增 6.8%，近三個月累計年增 6.4%；核
心零售月增 0.5%，消費支出仍具韌性。惟整體增幅放緩，後續須關注通
膨、利率及就業情勢變化。 
⚫ 11 月美國消費者信心指數由 48.0 升至 51.5，月增 7.3%，反映選後景氣
預期改善及汽油價格回落；短期與長期通膨預期分別降至 3.7%及 3.3%，
惟生活成本壓力仍存，整體信心仍偏弱。 
⚫ 10 月美國建築許可年化總數降至 129.5 萬戶，月減 7.4%；惟新屋開工
增至 130.9 萬戶，月增 9.9%，多戶住宅開工大幅回升。完工戶數月增
6.5%，顯示住宅供給持續釋出，惟高房貸利率仍抑制建商投資意願。 
⚫ 10 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示，美國經濟與勞動市場維持韌性，AI 投資持
續帶動經濟活動；惟 9 月 PCE 及核心 PCE 通膨率升至 3.6%及 3.1%，
通膨上行風險仍存。多數官員支持維持利率於 3.75%至 4.00%，但認為
未來仍可能進一步升息，並關切 AI 相關融資及金融穩定風險。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 美國 11 月中旬擴大 AI 晶片出口管制，將多國列為高風險轉運地並限制
中國取得海外算力，顯示管制重心由晶片延伸至運算能力，恐提高中國
AI 發展成本並加劇美中科技競爭。 
⚫ 輝達代理執行長 Colette Kress 表示，未來將不再向中國銷售 Hopper 系
列 AI 晶片，包括 H200 產品，且後續財測已排除相關營收；在美國持
續收緊 AI 晶片出口管制下，市場憂心恐衝擊資料中心業務表現，惟公
司認為先進 AI 晶片需求強勁，整體成長動能仍穩健。 
⚫ 美國總統川普、中國國家主席習近平及俄羅斯總統普丁於深圳舉行歷史
性三邊峰會，就全球安全、貿易、能源及科技治理等議題交換意見。外
界認為，此次會晤反映大國溝通協調需求升高，對未來國際政治經濟秩
序及地緣局勢發展具有重要指標意義。 
短評 
⚫ 美國 10 月零售銷售溫和成長，11 月消費者信心回升且通膨預期下降，
內需仍具韌性；惟建築許可持續下滑，反映房市復甦仍面臨壓力。另一
方面，聯準會對通膨風險維持警戒，AI 投資雖支撐經濟成長，但相關債

12 月再度啟動升息以抑制通膨。在高利率與銀行放款趨嚴，加上法國面
臨龐大財政赤字壓縮支出等逆風下，內需恐將持續承壓。

AI 帶動外銷訂單創新高 
經濟 ⚫ 10 月外銷訂單達 1,238.2 億美元，年增 78.3%。 
短評 
⚫ AI 相關需求持續擴張，不僅支撐資通及電子產品接單，亦逐步外溢至
基本金屬、機械等傳統產業。短期外銷接單動能仍可望維持強勁，惟在
高基期下，後續外銷訂單年增率將有所收斂。

一、 美國—消費動能維持韌性、房市復甦仍受壓抑，通膨風險與 AI 金融隱憂牽
動聯準會政策走向 
2026 年 10 月美國零售與餐飲服務銷售額經季節調整後為 7,482 億美元，較
上月增加 0.2%，年增 6.8%；近三月(8-10 月)累計銷售額年增 6.4%，消費支出仍
具支撐。若扣除汽車及零件銷售，零售額月增 0.6%；進一步扣除汽車、零件及加
油站後，核心零售銷售月增 0.5%，與前月持平。主要業別中，雜項零售商、非實
體零售商、加油站及健康與個人護理用品商店銷售成長較佳，餐飲服務亦維持正
成長；惟汽車及零件經銷商、家具及家居用品店與服飾配件店銷售下滑。整體而
言，10 月零售銷售增幅雖較上月放緩，但核心零售及服務消費表現穩健，顯示美
國內需仍具支撐力道，後續仍須關注通膨、利率及就業情勢變化對消費動能之影
響。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 11 月美國消費者信心指數終值由上月的 48.0 回升
至 51.5，月增 7.3%、年增 1.0%，惟仍較今年 2 月美伊衝突爆發前低約 9%，顯示
消費者信心雖有所改善，但整體仍處低檔。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由 46.7
升至 49.4，月增 5.8%、年減 3.3%；消費者預期指數則由 48.8 升至 52.9，月增
8.4%、年增 3.7%，反映民眾對未來經濟前景的看法有所改善。調查指出，期中選
舉後，消費者預期未來分裂政府格局可能帶來政策調整，推升短期及長期企業景
氣預期，其中獨立選民改善最為明顯，民主黨支持者亦有所回升，惟共和黨支持
者的預期則轉弱。此外，汽油價格下降使低資產及部分中等資產家庭的財務狀況
改善，但持股較多的家庭對個人財務狀況的評價則有所下降，與 AI 相關因素引
發的股市波動相呼應。通膨預期方面，未來一年通膨預期由 4.7%大幅降至 3.7%，
惟仍高於今年 2 月的 3.4%；長期通膨預期亦由 3.5%降至 3.3%，仍高於 2024 年
2.8%至 3.2%的區間，顯示通膨疑慮雖有所緩解，但尚未完全消除。 
2026 年 10 月美國住宅市場建築許可年化總數為 129.5 萬戶，較上月減少 7.4%、
較去年同期減少 8.7%，其中單戶住宅建築許可降至 77.2 萬戶，月減 8.7%；5 戶
以上多戶住宅建築許可則降至 47.5 萬戶，月減 5.2%，年減 0.4%，住宅開發規劃
仍趨審慎。另一方面，新屋開工年化數增至 130.9 萬戶，月增 9.9%、年增 2.8%，

其中單戶住宅開工增至 86.4 萬戶，月增 4.0%；5 戶以上多戶住宅開工升至 43.3

其中單戶住宅完工增至 90.0 萬戶，月增 6.3%，惟整體仍較去年同期減少 10.4%。
整體而言，美國 10 月住宅市場需求仍受高房貸利率及住房負擔能力壓力影響，
建築許可續呈下滑，惟新屋開工及完工表現改善，顯示住宅供給仍持續釋出，後
續仍須關注房貸利率走勢、住宅需求及營建成本變化對房市發展之影響。 
2026 年 10 月 FOMC 會議紀要重點如下：(1)第三季 GDP 成長動能增強，高
科技產品進口及 AI 相關投資維持熱絡；(2)勞動市場整體穩定並接近充分就業狀
態，失業率維持於 4.2%，薪資成長較前一年放緩；(3)9 月 PCE 及核心 PCE 通膨
率分別升至 3.6%及 3.1%，能源價格上漲、先前關稅措施及 AI 投資帶動之需求與
供給壓力，持續使通膨高於 2%目標，多數官員認為短期內上行風險仍偏高；(4)
多數官員支持將聯邦基金利率目標區間維持於 3.75%至 4.00%，以評估前次升息
及金融條件變化對經濟之影響，惟幾乎所有官員均認為未來仍可能進一步升息，
以確保通膨回落至目標水準；(5)官員普遍關切 AI 相關債務快速擴張、複雜融資
及循環融資安排可能引發之估值修正風險，並持續關注避險基金槓桿、銀行對非
銀行金融機構之間接曝險，以及網路安全等潛在金融穩定議題。 
美國川普政府於 11 月 16 日宣布進一步收緊中國取得輝達（Nvidia）等企業
先進人工智慧（AI）晶片的管道，將新加坡、泰國、馬來西亞、印尼及巴西列為
高風險轉運國並要求出口許可，同時限制向中國企業提供大量算力的海外資料中
心取得受管制晶片。此舉正值美國總統川普前往亞洲、並於 11 月 19 日在深圳與
中國國家主席習近平會晤前後，凸顯華府在防堵晶片轉運與遠端算力租用漏洞上
的政策動向。相較過去主要限制實體晶片出口，新措施進一步強化對海外資料中
心及 AI 算力使用的監管，反映美國持續將管制重點由硬體設備延伸至運算能力
本身，以限制中國取得高階 AI 模型訓練與推理所需資源。市場預期短期內恐提
高中國科技企業取得先進算力的成本與難度，並加速中國發展國產 AI 晶片及自
主算力基礎設施；另一方面，美中科技競爭進一步升溫，後續相關出口管制範圍、
執行細節及北京的反制措施，將持續牽動全球半導體、雲端服務及 AI 產業供應
鏈布局。

美國政府核准出口的 Hopper 系列資料中心產品，包括 H200 人工智慧晶片，此舉
被視為輝達對中國市場策略的重要轉變。輝達第三季仍向中國銷售約 24 億美元
Hopper 產品，但未來財測已不再納入相關出貨收入；同時，公司宣布成立董事會
層級委員會，負責監督國家安全政策、出口管制合規及全球貿易風險。在美國近
期進一步強化對中國 AI 晶片及跨境算力服務管制之際，市場關注新措施對輝達
營運的影響。投資銀行 Jefferies 估計，新規可能使輝達明年資料中心業務營收減
少 440 億至 620 億美元；不過公司認為，受惠於 Vera Rubin 等先進 AI 晶片需求
強勁，整體營收成長動能仍可望維持穩健。 
美國總統川普、中國國家主席習近平及俄羅斯總統普丁於深圳舉行歷史性三
邊高峰會，成為近年國際政治最受矚目的外交活動之一。此次會談聚焦全球安全
情勢、經貿合作、能源供應、AI 治理、科技競爭及區域衝突等重大議題，並就俄
烏戰爭、中東局勢、美中貿易關係、半導體出口管制及全球供應鏈穩定等議題交
換意見。市場尤其關注美中雙方是否於關稅措施、科技管制及投資限制等爭議領
域釋出緩和訊號，以及美俄是否就地緣安全與能源市場穩定達成進一步共識。分
析人士指出，本次峰會反映主要大國透過高層直接對話管控分歧、降低衝突風險
的意願升高，亦凸顯全球治理架構正朝多極化方向發展。儘管三方在烏克蘭戰爭、
台海安全、軍事部署及高科技競爭等核心議題上仍存在明顯立場差異，短期內達
成實質突破的難度仍高，惟此次會晤有助維持主要大國溝通管道，並為後續經貿、
能源及安全事務協調奠定基礎。峰會成果及相關聯合聲明內容，將持續受到國際
社會、金融市場及企業界高度關注，其後續發展亦可能對全球政治經濟秩序產生
深遠影響。 
二、歐元區—工業生產、營造表現皆暫時回溫 
 歐元區 9 月工業生產月增 0.2%，中止連續三個月的衰退，主要是車廠開始回
補汽車庫存，帶動汽車生產反彈，使耐久消費財產出月增 1.3%，抵銷因零售需求
降溫，導致能源(包含燃油等)、非耐久消費財產出月減 1.7%、0.4%的影響。另外，
9 月營造業產出回升 0.1%，中止連續四個月的衰退局面，不過，營造業持續面臨
能源與建材成本上漲，加上法國與西班牙等核心經濟體的房建與公共預算受限，
使營造業的疲軟局面暫時難以扭轉。 
 儘管歐元區 9 月工業與營造業產出止跌回升，但復甦基礎仍受到國際政經不

三、日本—出口持續改善，通膨壓力強化升息預期 
 9 月核心機械訂單(扣除波動訂單)月減 0.3%，第三季核心機械訂單季增 1.2%，
連續第四季成長，顯示企業資本支出動能仍然穩健。其中製造業是主要成長引擎，
AI 與半導體需求正逐步朝更廣泛的製造業擴散，化學產品及商用機械訂單分別成
長 57.0%及 80.5%，反映半導體材料及設備需求依然旺盛。相較之下，非製造業
資本支出動能較為溫和，反映成本上升導致企業調整部分投資計畫，惟勞動市場
結構性問題仍將支撐自動化與生產力相關投資。整體而言，日本資本支出週期尚
未出現明顯轉弱跡象，在 AI 與半導體投資外溢效應帶動下，預期資本支出仍可
維持穩健成長。 
 10 月出口年增 20.5%，其中汽車出口成長 20.0%，成為主要推動力，反映先
前災害造成的供應中斷逐步消退；半導體相關出口成長 49.7%，對中國以外的亞
洲市場科技產品出口更創歷史新高，顯示 AI 與資本支出相關需求持續強勁。從
地區來看，對歐盟、美國及中國出口均成長，其中對美出口已回升至 2025 年 2 月
以來高點。整體而言，出口動能持續改善，汽車出口復甦、AI、資本支出相關需
求可望成為後續出口的重要支撐。 
 10 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.3%，顯示通膨動能持續增強，主要受到
石油產品價格上漲持續轉嫁至商品價格，且漲價壓力已由商品逐步擴散至食品及
服務。雖然政府釋出大米儲備、高中免學費及能源補貼等措施壓低部分價格，使
米價及學費明顯下跌，但剔除生鮮食品後的核心通膨仍達 2.2%，高於央行 2%的
通膨目標。展望未來，隨汽油補貼自 12 月起逐步縮減，加上食品業者可能於 2027
年 4 月消費稅減免生效前提前調漲價格，預期 2027 年通膨壓力將進一步升高，
進而促使日本央行持續升息。

展望未來，房地產的調整週期可能會延續至 2028 年後，加上去產能政策與供
應鏈外移壓抑民間投資，消費與投資動能料將維持疲軟。在出口方面，近期 APEC
峰會促成中加、中澳經貿破冰並推進亞太自貿區，有助支撐出口表現。然而，美
中貿易關係在川習會後依然沒有重大突破，明年一月美中關稅休戰期滿，仍為貿
易環境帶來不確定因素。 
五、印度及東南亞—泰國 2026 年第三季 GDP2.9%；印尼央行升息以穩定匯價 
(1)新加坡：10 月非石油國內出口（NODX）年增 79.8%，連續第 24 個月成長並

上近期因輝達黃仁勳桃色風波，美國商務部將新加坡列入晶片出口管制國家，恐
影響後續出口表現。

用相關內容。

9

(2)印尼：印尼央行利率會議意外升息 1 碼(BI rate)至 6%，貸款利率和隔夜存款利
率分別調高 1 碼至 6.75%和 5.25%，旨在全球經濟情勢也充滿不確定性、印尼貨
幣面臨新一輪壓力下，採取先發制人動作，力求匯率穩定並使通膨率控制在
2.5%±1%的目標區間內，央行同時維持對 2026 年 GDP 成長 4.9%至 5.7%的預測。
展望未來，維持外匯穩定仍是印尼央行的首要政策重點，在通膨居高不下、美國
聯準會升息、美元走強，資金外流壓力仍高下，未來仍有進一步升息風險。 
(3)泰國：2026 年第三季經濟成長率(GDP)年增 2.9%，主要得益於全球對人工智慧
相關產品的強勁需求，帶動出口大幅成長 18.3%，同時，進口成長放緩至 16.3%，
放大淨出口對經濟的貢獻；私人消費（2.5%）和固定投資（11.8%）成長速度加
快，惟政府支出四個季度以來首次下降（-1.6%）。按產業別來看，製造業產出成
長 3.7%為主要貢獻來源、運輸和倉儲業（3.1%）、批發和零售業（6.0%）的產出
成長加速，但旅遊業依然疲軟。泰國政府將今年的 GDP 成長預期從先前的 2.0%-
2.5%上調至 2.5%，高於 2025 年的 2.4%。 
(4)馬來西亞：10 月出口額年增 61.1%，達 MYR 2,392 億元的歷史新高，持續反
映 AI 帶動的強勁需求，電子電氣產品出口增幅達 87.2%，礦產品出口亦大幅成長
37.0%，抵銷農產品出口下降 6.1%的影響，對美國、台灣、新加坡出口分別激增
108%、293%、55%。10 月進口額年增 54.4%至 MYR 1,974 億元，其中，中間產
品（64.0%）、資本貨物（32.9%）、消費品（11.8%）等進口均有成長，按產業別
觀之，製造業、礦業進口額分別成長 60.6%、44.2%，本月自印度（119.6%）、韓
國（194.0%）進口呈倍數成長，自中國、東協進口亦成長 56.5%、64.8%。展望未
來，美國商務部 11/16 發布新規，將新加坡、馬來西亞、泰國、印尼及巴西等五
國列為晶片轉運高風險國家，要求先進 AI 晶片出口至上述國家的部分數據中心
須事先取得許可，並進一步限制中國企業透過海外數據中心遠端租用算力。由於
馬來西亞資料中心容量多屬「共置（colocation）」型態，中國企業占比逾半數，
一旦中國企業難以透過馬國及周邊國家租用高階算力，將影響中國企業投資意
願，加上合規成本增加，將影響未來出口及經濟表現。

レポート

2026 年冬のボーナス見通し

～企業の好業績と人手不足を背景に堅調な増加が続く～

調査部 主席研究員 小林 真一郎 
研究員 小林 啓介

○ 2026 年冬の民間企業（調査産業計・事業所規模 5 人以上）のボーナスは、前年比＋5.0%と 6 年連続で増
加が見込まれる。好調な企業業績と堅調な雇用情勢が追い風となり、大幅な伸びになるだろう。 
○ 支給労働者割合は 87.3%（前年差＋0.8%ポイント）と 2 年ぶりに上昇するだろう。同割合はコロナ前の水準
を超えて回復しており、雇用者数の増加が続く中、ボーナスが支給される事業所で働く労働者の数は 4,577
万人（前年比＋1.9%）と、4 年連続で過去最多を更新する見込みである。 
○ 一人当たり支給額と支給労働者数の増加を受け、ボーナスの支給総額は 20.4 兆円（前年比＋7.0%）と 6 年
連続で増加しよう。ボーナスの支給総額の伸びは物価上昇率を大きく上回っており、個人消費の回復を下
支えすると期待される。 
○ 2026 年冬の国家公務員（管理職および非常勤を除く一般行政職）のボーナス（期末・勤勉手当）の平均支
給額は 77 万 9,100 円（前年比＋4.4%）と前年冬に続き、大きめの増加が見込まれる。景気回復に伴う民間
給与の増加が公務員にも反映される状況が続き、基本給、ボーナス支給月数とも引き上げられるとみられる。 
2026 年冬のボーナス見通し

の支給を受けていない労働者も含む）の平均

含む）の数

1. 2026 年夏のボーナス ～企業の好業績を背景に 5 年連続の増加

厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計調査」によると、民間企業（調査産業計・事業所規模 5 人以上）における 2026
年夏のボーナスの一人当たり支給額は、前年比＋5.3%と 5 年連続で増加した（図表 1）。前年（2025 年）の
伸びである同＋2.9%を大きく上回っており、2026 年は企業の好業績を背景にボーナスは堅調に増加した。 
業種別では、製造業（前年比＋5.8%）、非製造業（同＋5.3%）ともに増加した。内訳をみると、生活関連サ
ービス等（前年比＋25.7%）、建設業（同＋14.4%）、学術研究等（同＋12.9%）などが大幅に増加し、全体
の数字を押し上げた。一方で、複合サービス事業（同－2.1%）は減少した。 
ボーナス支給月数（所定内給与に対する比率）は 1.10 カ月分と前年から 0.02 カ月増加した。また、ボー
ナスが支給された労働者の割合 1は 85.1%（前年差＋0.5%ポイント）と小幅に上昇した。雇用者数が順調に
増加して過去最高水準を更新しており、ボーナスが支給された労働者数は前年比＋1.4%と増加した。夏の
ボーナスの支給総額（平均給与額×支給労働者数）は 19.9 兆円（前年比＋6.8%）と過去最高を記録した。

図表 1 夏のボーナス実績：平均支給額（前年比）と支給月数

（注 2）支給月数は所定内給与に対する支給割合 
（出所）厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計」

める割合。

2. 2026 年冬のボーナス見通し

(1) 民間企業 ～ 企業の好業績と労働需給の逼迫を追い風に、6 年連続で増加する見込み

厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計調査」ベースで見た民間企業（調査産業計・事業所規模 5 人以上）の 2026 年冬
のボーナスの一人当たり平均支給額は 44 万 6,252 円（前年比＋5.0%）と 6 年連続での増加を予想する（図表
2）。企業規模を問わず、製造業、非製造業とも一人当たり平均支給額は増加すると予測する。 
今年の冬のボーナスが順調な増加を続けるとみられる背景に、企業業績の好調さと雇用情勢の堅調さがある。
企業の経常利益（全規模、金融保険業を除く全産業、季節調整値）は、2026 年 4～6 月期に過去最高を更新す
るなど好調に推移している。今後イラン情勢の展開次第では業績に下押し圧力がかかるリスクはあるが、冬のボ
ーナスへの影響は軽微だろう。 
また、人手不足は深刻化し、労働需給は逼迫している。日銀短観における雇用人員判断 DI は対面型サービ
ス業を中心に大幅な「不足」超が続き、企業の人手不足感が強まる中、完全失業率は 2％台半ばと低水準での推
移が続いている。

（注 1）調査産業計、事業所規模 5 人以上  
（注 2）支給月数は所定内給与に対する支給割合 
（出所）厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計」

(2) 公務員 ～ 基本給、支給月数ともプラスに寄与し、ボーナスは 5 年連続で増加

内閣人事局の発表によると、国家公務員（管理職および非常勤を除く一般行政職）の 2026 年夏のボーナス
（期末・勤勉手当）は 73 万 8,500 円（前年比＋4.5%）と 4 年連続で増加した。昨年の人事院勧告に基づく給与
法改正でボーナスの基準となる基本給が前年比＋3.3%と増加したほか、ボーナス支給月数が昨夏から 0.025 カ
月引き上げられた。 
2026 年冬のボーナスの平均支給額は 77 万 9,100 円（前年比＋4.4%）と、5 年連続で増加すると予測する（図
表 8）。今年の人事院勧告に沿った給与法改正が行われることを前提に、平均給与額が増加（＋3.3%）するととも
に、ボーナス支給月数が昨冬から 0.025 カ月分（勤勉手当、期末手当がそれぞれ 0.0125 カ月分）引き上げられ
ると想定する。支給額は夏冬通じて過去最高を更新し、景気回復に伴う民間給与の増加が公務員にも反映され
る状況が続くとみられる。今後も、民間の賃金上昇傾向が続く中、公務員のボーナスも高めの伸びが続く見込み
である。 
図表 8 冬のボーナス予測（国家公務員）

### Page 1

Technology - Asia-Pacific (H)

ABF substrate & CCL: making the case for 
limited downside after valuation reset

Price Objective Change

Valuation implies sizable 2028 EPS downside   
Broadly speaking, share prices of the ABF substrate and CCL stocks under our Greater 
China coverage fell by 40-50% from their October peaks to their November lows. Based 
on the two scenarios presented in this report, we believe current valuations may imply 
the market’s over-concerns about the inflection of AI server cycle or a slowdown in 
capex growth. Using closing price as of Nov. 16th, the stocks traded at 7-13x 2028E P/E 
under scenario 1, in which we assume a 15% shortfall in AI-related revenue vs. our 
estimates (Exhibit 3). These multiples are below the midpoint of their valuation range 
before the rise of AI server demand from mid-2023. Under scenario 2, we divide the 
current share price by our long-term anchored P/E multiple to calculate the 2028E EPS 
imlied by the share price. Based on our analysis, current valuations imply a 40-90% 
shortfall (or discount) in AI-related revenue versus our estimates (Exhibit 4). 
Raise estimates but lower P/E multiples  
For 2027-28E OP, we raise our estimates by 4-9%/19-31%/3-6%/19-24%/22-23%/32-
33% for Unimicron/Kinsus/NYPCB/EMC/TUC/Shengyi, reflecting a general beat in 3Q26 
GM and ongoing price hike outlook across ABF/BT substrate and CCL. However, we 
lower our POs on these names (Exhibit 1) as we lower P/E multiples by ~30% to reflect 
sector-wide de-rating in AI server supply chain, while the valuation period is unchanged. 
See Exhibit 10 for details of the changes in valuation multiples.  
Stay largely positive on ABF substrate and CCL sectors 
Despite lowered POs and multiples, our positive stance on the ABF substrate sector is 
unchanged, reflecting our expectation of a more sustained supply deficit through 2028 
as well as the T-glass price hike by Nittobo (see report) and Resonac, which should set 
the stage for substrate makers to lift ASP further. We thus reiterate Buy on Unimicron, 
Kinsus and NYPCB. As for CCL, we are also generally positive on the S/D outlook given 
the lead time of certain equipment is still at least 1.5-2 years, yet at single stock level 
we are more constructive on Buy-rated names including 1) EMC with at least 70-80% 
revenue mix from M7-grade and above in 2027-28 and its estimated 50-60% share of 
the AI-related high-end CCL market, along with potential exposure to M10 adoption in 
SpaceX’s AI server rack based on our channel checks; and 2) TUC being one of the few 
high-end CCL suppliers with more aggressive capacity expansion during 2026-30 (~3x 
over the period). We also see potential for additional ~2mn sheet/month capacity 
addition in 2030 (in Thailand).   For discussions on ABF substrate S/D model update and 
our thoughts on CPO impact, see page 2.

### Page 2

ABF substrate – wider undersupply expected vs previously  
We update ABF substrate industry S/D model with this report, and we now expect 
7%/18%/20% under-supply (was 7%/14%/19%) in 2026/27/28 thanks to the raised TAM 
(see report, Core Wars: raising CPU TAM to $240bn, still early stages of agents) for AI 
accelerators and CPUs (central processing units), which leads us to estimate 36%/24% 
(was 32%/26%) demand growth by the industry in 2027/28. After reflecting the demand 
updates, AI accelerators are expected to account for 41%/52%/55% (was 
40%/46%/51%) of demand in 2026/27/28. Overall, our industry S/D model also 
considers the 2.5x SAP (semi additive process) demand growth across 2026-30 shared 
by Ibiden, which incorporates 1.5x and 4.4x demand growth by server CPU and AI 
accelerators respectively over the same period.  
Thoughts on CPO impact for CCL/PCB 
Our checks with supply chain suggest that many of the CCL materials are upgraded to 
M9-grade (based on low Dk2 cloth and hyper very low profile 4 copper foil), when 
pluggable optical modules are upgraded to OBO (on-board optics) and/or NPO (near 
package optics). In terms of CCL-grade vs PCB layer count, there is a general 
categorization in AI server and adjacent applications shown in Exhibit 12, and it indicates 
that M8-grade or above CCL will be required when the board layer count moves up to 30 
or higher. Further, given that CPO gen-2 with OOI (optics on interposer) is unlikely to 
ramp before 2028, alongside the upgrade in transmission to 3.2T, CCL material used in 
mainboard may have already been upgraded to M10-grade by then under a non- 
CPO/OOI architecture. This means CPO/OOI adoption may only result in a material 
downgrade to M8/M9-grade.

BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

Price objective basis & risk 
Elite Material (ETMCF) 
Our PO of NT$8,200 is based on 30x 2H27-1H28E P/E, at the mid-to-high-end of its 
historical range of 6-34x since 2016. In our view, the multiple is appropriate, given the 
market-share expansion in server/networking, where we see structural growth in 
addressable market.

Upside risks are (1) share gain at substrate CCL, (2) stronger 5G smartphone shipment, 
(3) greater 5G smartphone spec upgrades, and (4) favorable FX and raw material cost 
change.

### Page 13

Downside risks are (1) share loss at iPhone, (2) keener competition in high-end CCL, and 
(3) unfavorable FX and raw material cost change. 
Kinsus (KNSUF) 
We set our PO at NT$1,450 based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at mid-to-high-end 
level in its historical trading range (8-40x) since 2013. In our view, the multiple is backed 
by ROE and operating margin recovery to 36% and 25% in 2027-28E (vs 8% and 10% in 
2025-26E).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) increasing adoption of SiP and AiP, 
(3) lower competition pressure and (4) slow development of alternative technology.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) muted SiP/AiP adoption, (3) 
more severe competition, and (4) unfavorable technology development. 
NYPCB (NANYF) 
Our PO of NT$2,000 is based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E. We view this multiple as fair 
given ROE and operating margin expansion to 39% and 41% in 2027-28E (vs 13% and 
14% in 2025-26E).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) higher SiP/AiP adoption, and (3) 
less intensified competition pressure in PCB business.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) muted SiP/AiP adoption, 
and (3) more severe competition in PCB business. 
Taiwan Union Technology Corporation (TWUNF) 
Our PO NT$2,600 is based on 27x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at the mid-to-high-end of 
the historical trading range of 8-31x during 2016-2026. In our view, the valuation 
multiple is justified, given operating margin and ROE expansion toward 26% and 63% in 
2026-28E (vs 13% and 16% in 2023-25) along with robust free-cash flow generation in 
over the same period.

Downside risks are 
1) Fluctuation in raw material prices, which could lead to a more volatile gross margin 
for TUC in the near term 
2) More intensified competition in CCL, which could pressure TUC's overall margin 
profile 
3) Adoption of new type of upstream material (e.g., quartz), which could bring business 
uncertainty for the names in CCL industry, including TUC 
4) Worse-than-expected execution by the company, which could cause market-share loss 
and/or decreased business traction with customers 
Unimicron (UMCRF) 
Our PO of NT$1,650 is based on 35x 2H27-1H28E, which is roughly 1 standard deviation 
above the historical average of 20x since 2017. In our view, the multiple is appropriate, 
as we expect Unimicron's earnings to be supported by its high-end technology offerings, 
where end market demand seems more resilient.

Upside risks are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) greater PC and regular server demand, and (3) 
lower competition pressure.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) lower ASP content increase 
at AI server, and (3) bigger drop in the demand of consumer electronic products.

of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment rating 
of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, the 0-
12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a stock 
should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation).

### Page 1

• AST2700 transition and pricing. AST2700 represents ASPEED’s most 
important product-cycle driver. Management expects shipments to exceed 
10 million units in 2027 and believes AST2700 could account for more than 
half of its major BMC mix in 2028. Indicative ASP is approximately 50% 
above current AST2600. ASPEED implemented an approximately 10% price 
increase from December 1, 2026, following another increase earlier in the 
year. The latest adjustment is intended to offset higher foundry and other 
supplier costs and applies to products shipped after the effective date. 
Because BMCs represent a very small proportion of total server or rack 
costs, management does not expect the increase to materially affect 
customer purchasing decisions. Market-share gains at hyperscale 
customers and continued migration toward AST2700 should allow blended 
ASP to rise even if unit growth moderates. Supply conditions are expected 
to improve around mid-2027, enabling shipments to align more closely 
with underlying customer demand.
• Product mix and GM outlook. Peripheral controllers have expanded faster 
than management initially expected and may now represent 15–20% of 
demand, compared with the previous 10% assumption, per management. 
Growth is coming from networking switches, cooling systems and other 
infrastructure surrounding compute servers. The new AST1840 solution 
combines ASPEED’s management controller with a Lattice FPGA, adding

programmability, hardware security and a smaller footprint for BMC add-in cards. These higher-function products
should carry ASPs above first-generation devices. The GM outlook depends on product mix and cost pass-through
rather than pricing alone. Management indicated that rising input costs will limit the immediate GM benefit of price
increases, but richer AST2700 and peripheral mix should support longer-term profitability.

• Architecture exposure, market turbulence and risks. BMC content varies by system. Management offered rough
planning ratios of approximately one BMC per GPU in certain NVIDIA configurations, one BMC for every three
accelerators in some ASIC platforms, and one BMC per one or two CPUs in conventional servers. More switches,
cooling subsystems and power-management elements could also increase management-controller content even if
the number of compute racks changes. While there is some concern on a solution for IMC  (Infrastructure

Management Controller), ASPEED believes that rack-level control remains fragmented among dedicated servers,
switches, power-rack controllers and software, thus board-level BMC functions cannot be replaced because they
must operate independently when the host processor or operating system fails. Overall, despite rising investor
concerns on customer inventory accumulation and delayed platform deployments, ASPEED's diversification across
GPU, ASIC, CPU and peripheral applications keeps its growth outlook positive.

Prepared for Kevin Lu

### Page 3

Aspeed Technology (5274.TWO)
25 November 2026 Citi Research
3
Aspeed Technology
Valuation
We value Aspeed at NT$20,700 based on a historical mid-cycle multiple (since 2020) of 50x average 2027E-28E EPS, as we
believe it fairly reflects the company’s earnings performance and industry dynamics, secular growth trends, and Aspeed’s
strong supply chain position, as well as robust earnings growth amid margin expansions.
Risks
Our quant rating system rates the Aspeed stock High Risk based on its volatility over the past year, which we think is

unwarranted given the company's dominance and outlook in the BMC sector. Key downside risks to our earnings forecasts and
target price include: 1) weaker-than-expected growth in servers; 2) slower-than-expected CPU platform migration; 3) peer
competition; and 4) limited progress in product diversification.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

Document 173: 410亿美元：制药业近三年最大并购的风向

Source PDF: all report pdf/410亿美元：制药业近三年最大并购的风向.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

赛诺菲以410亿美元收购Insmed，获得两款商业化药物及III期管线TPIP，成为辉瑞收购
Seagen以来制药⾏业最⼤规模并购。交易背后，是Dupixent即将⾯临专利悬崖，赛诺菲亟
需寻找增⻓接⼒棒。这也释放出⾏业信号：⼤型药企正以⾼溢价换取商业化资产，Biotech
的估值逻辑正从管线潜⼒转向现⾦流确定性。
摘要：
11⽉24⽇，赛诺菲宣布以每股175美元的全现⾦对价收购美国⽣物制药公司Insmed，总对价410
亿美元（约376亿欧元）。这是⾃2023年12⽉辉瑞以430亿美元收购Seagen之后，全球制药⾏业
规模最⼤的⼀笔并购。
市场对这个消息并不意外。收购传闻流出后，Insmed股价已经被推⾼了⼀轮；消息正式落地当
天，Insmed盘前再涨约14%，赛诺菲股价上涨近6%。买⽅和卖⽅的股东同时投出赞成票，这在
⼤型并购⾥不算常⻅。
当我们把交易条款、资产清单和赛诺菲⾃⼰的财务数据放在⼀起看，这笔并购的轮廓便慢慢清
晰起来。
三块资产
赛诺菲买到⼿的东西⼤致分三个部分：
图：赛诺菲收购Insmed获得的三块资产。来源：赛诺菲新闻稿、Insmed

第⼀块是Brinsupri（brensocatib）。
这是⼀款每⽇⼀次的⼝服DPP1抑制剂，2025年8⽉在美国上市，⽤于⾮囊性纤维化⽀⽓管扩张
症（NCFB），⽬前已在美国、欧盟、英国和⽇本获批。它是全球⾸个获批的DPP1抑制剂，也是
⾸个且唯⼀获批⽤于NCFB的治疗药物，在这个适应症⾥，它没有对⼿。
2025年8⽉上市到2026年9⽉30⽇，累计营收10.9亿美元，上市⼀年多就跨过重磅炸弹⻔槛。
Insmed⾸席执⾏官Will Lewis的说法是，Brinsupri在上市⼀年多时间⾥已经成为NCFB患者的标
准治疗。
第⼆块是Arikayce。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

阿⽶卡星脂质体吸⼊混悬液，联合多药⽅案治疗难治性⻦分枝杆菌复合体（MAC）肺病，是
美、欧、⽇唯⼀获批的针对治疗难治⼈群的吸⼊疗法。
2025年全年营收4.338亿美元，同⽐增⻓19%；今年9⽉，FDA⼜以优先审评受理了它的⼀项新
适应症申请。这款药的定位是慢病化的⼩体量资产：增速稳定，天花板不⾼，但现⾦流可⻅。
第三块是III期管线TPIP。
曲前列尼尔棕榈酸酯吸⼊粉雾剂，每⽇⼀次吸⼊的前列腺素类在研药物，针对肺动脉⾼压和纤
维化肺病等多种罕⻅呼吸适应症。这是交易⾥⻛险最⾼的部分，也是赛诺菲愿意为“中期增⻓”⽀
付对价的原因之⼀。
交易结构上，410亿美元全现⾦，由⾃有资⾦加债务融资完成，摩根⼤通与法国巴黎银⾏提供了
350亿美元的过渡贷款。
溢价从何⽽来
按消息传出前的股价计算，赛诺菲⽀付了约46%的溢价（较上⼀交易⽇收盘价的溢价约17.4%，
传闻阶段的抢跑推⾼了基数）。
呼吸罕⻅病是⼀个特殊的赛道。NCFB和难治性MAC肺病都是慢性、进⾏性疾病，患者⼀旦开始
治疗，⽤药周期以年计，停药和换药的空间很⼩，患者粘性很强。Brinsupri作为该适应症全球

唯⼀的获批药物，竞争格局在可预⻅的窗⼝内没有直接对⼿。
这类“孤⼉药加慢病”的组合，对应的正是资本市场最愿意给定价的属性：现⾦流可⻅度⾼。
Arikayce连续19%的增速和Brinsupri⼀年⼗亿美元的放量曲线，都是这种可⻅度的注脚。
需要说明的是，这类资产同时也意味着患者池有天花板。NCFB的患病⼈群规模有限，Brinsupri
的增⻓依赖渗透率爬坡和地区扩展，⽽⾮患者池扩张。Insmed管理层给出的⼝径是，商业化产
品加III期管线合计具有“数⼗亿美元级的峰值销售潜⼒”。
专利悬崖的压迫
赛诺菲为什么此刻出⼿，答案在Dupixent的财务报表⾥。
2026年第三季度，Dupixent单季销售额57亿欧元，占公司当季净销售额接近四成；2025年全
年，这款药销售超过150亿欧元。⼀款药物撑起⼀家欧洲最⼤药企的近半收⼊，这种集中度在⼤
型MNC⾥⼏乎没有第⼆例。⽽它的美国专利预计在2031年前后到期。

### Page 3

图：Dupixent占赛诺菲净销售额的⽐重。来源：赛诺菲2026年三季报
专利到期意味着什么，⾏业已经反复演⽰过：仿制药进⼊，价格体系坍塌，收⼊在两三年内滑
掉⼤部分。留给赛诺菲的时间窗，是从现在到2031年之前，⼤约三到五年。药物从临床到上市
需要⼗年上下的周期，如果靠⾃研，时间上已经来不及。并购是唯⼀能把“接棒资产”提前锁进时
间表的⽅式。
这样的基本事实决定了交易的设计逻辑。Brinsupri已经在放量，Arikayce提供稳定现⾦流，两块
资产现在就能并⼊损益表；TPIP的III期读出⼤约落在2027—2028年，正好接在交割之后。
如果⼀切顺利，到2031年Dupixent仿制药进场时，赛诺菲⼿⾥已经有⼀组年销售数⼗亿美元的
呼吸资产在运转。
⻛向：M&A补充管线的周期
把镜头拉远，这笔交易透露的⻛向⽐交易本⾝更值得关注。
图：2023年以来制药业⼤额并购序列。来源：Reuters、公司公告
辉瑞/Seagen（430亿美元，2023年12⽉）之后，⾏业整整三年没有出现同量级的并购。这三年

⾥，MNC的策略重⼼先后放在减肥药竞赛、内部管线重整和早期BD⼩额交易上。
赛诺菲/Insmed打破了沉寂，⽽且打破的⽅式带有明确的信号意义：买⽅已经退⽽求其次，不再
执着增⻓故事，更重要的是买到确定性——已经上市的药、已经跑出来的销售曲线、已经获批
的适应症。
这个选择的“对照组”是⾃研。⼀款新药从⽴项到上市平均需要⼗年左右，临床失败率超过九成；
⽽买⼀个已经商业化的资产组合，付出40%—50%的溢价，可以把失败⻛险和周期⻛险⼀并转
移掉。
在专利悬崖集中到来、投资者对“专利到期后还有没有增⻓”的追问越来越紧的环境⾥，MNC⽤溢
价换确定性的意愿系统性上升。赛诺菲的350亿美元过渡贷款也说明，融资环境给了他们这样做
的空间，利率环境宽松，资本市场对⾼评级药企的⼤额并购融资保持开放。
值得留意的是这个⻛向的传导路径。当MNC开始为“已商业化的呼吸罕⻅病资产”⽀付410亿美
元，它同时给整个市场的同类资产定了价：有患者粘性、竞争格局⼲净、现⾦流可⻅的细分领

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

域⻰头，估值锚点被抬⾼了。
这个定价信号会传向两个⽅向：传向⼀⼆级市场对呼吸、罕⻅病赛道的重新评估，也传向正在
寻找退出和兑现路径的Biotech。
对研发型企业⽽⾔，这意味着⼀个被并购的窗⼝正在打开；对投资⽅⽽⾔，这意味着资产筛选
标准正在从“管线想象⼒”向“现⾦流确定性”迁移。

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 11
Status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
아직 수요의 끝은 보이지 않는다 
AI 투자에 대한 우려의 목소리도 있지만 정작 부품을 공급하고 투자하는 쪽의 목소
리는 시간이 갈수록 더 커지고 있다. 공급 부족의 시점 매 분기 뒤로 밀려나고 있기 
때문이다. 28년까지 공급부족이라고 했던 메모리업체들은 이구동성으로 30년까지 공
급부족이 지속된다고 언급하고 있다. 이러한 변화는 실적에 고스란히 반영되고 있
다. 26년 3분기 메모리 영업이익만 108.5조원으로 추정한다. 4분기에는 140조원에 
이를 것으로 전망한다. 
HBM4와 QLC는 차별화 포인트 
삼성전자는 HBM에서 입지가 좋았던 적이 없었다. 점유율, 수익성, 가동률, 제품 성
능, 고객 등 모든 항목에서 경쟁사 대비 열위에 있었다. HBM4는 모든 항목에서 입
지를 개선시킬 변수가 될 것으로 예상한다. 26년 3분기 물량은 경쟁사 수준일 것으
로 보이고 연간 물량도 유사한 수준일 전망이다. 27년에는 가격 인상과 가동률 상승
으로 수익성도 크게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. QLC는 TLC와 함께 AI 모델 진화의 
수혜를 받을 제품으로 빠르게 부상하고 있다. 메타의 Muse 영향이다. 접속자당 가
상 메모리를 NAND에 100GB를 할당하고 있기 때문이다.  
부족한 주주환원으로 투자 우선 순위에서 밀릴 듯 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 
변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, HBM 
시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 
판단하기 때문이다. 목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황
을 고려하면 적정 주가는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치
에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 
제한적일 것으로 판단하기 때문이다.

삼성전자 (005930)

2 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 3분기 매출액은 195.3조원 
삼성전자의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.9% 증가한 195.3조원이다. VD/
가전을 제외한 주요 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 증가했다. DS, MX 모두 물량보다는 
가격 효과가 더 크게 작용한 것으로 분석된다. 사업부별로는  
1) DS 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 15.4% 증가한 147.1조원이다. 메모리
는 26년 2분기 대비 16.4% 증가했다. 26년 3분기 DRAM B/G(Bit Growth)
는 +2.0%, ASP는 +20.0%이다. NAND B/G는 +5.0%, ASP는 +18.0%이다. 
HBM 물량 증가는 HBM4가 주도했고, 그 이하 제품 비중은 하락한 것으로 
추정한다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 7.1% 증가한 8조원이다. 소형
은 해외 고객 및 국내 고객의 신제품 출시 영향으로 ASP 상승, 물량 증가 
효과가 동반되었고, 대형은 게이밍 모니터 비중을 확대했다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.4% 증가한 37.7조원이
다. MX는 15.5% 증가했다. 물량은 소폭 감소했고, ASP 상승 영향을 받았다. 
네트워크 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 60% 감소했다.  
4) VD/가전 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 4.1% 감소한 13.9조원이다. VD 
매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 6.5% 감소했다. 가전은 26년 2분기 대비 1.3% 
감소했다.

삼성전자의 26년 3분기 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 20% 증가한 107.4조원이다. DS,  
디스플레이와 MX는 2분기 대비 증가했고, VD/가전은 2분기 대비 감소했다. 사업부별
로는 
1) DS 사업부는 26년 2분기 대비 19.4% 증가한 106.5조원이다. 2분기 대비 영

비메모리는 영업적자를 유지했다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 54.5% 증가한 1.1조원이다.

익성이 개선되었다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자폭이 감소했다. 물량
은 줄었지만 High End 비중 상승으로 ASP는 개선되었고, 원가 비중이 낮아
진 영향이다. 
4) VD/가전 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자 규모가 커졌다.

6 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 4분기 매출액은 226.5조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 16.0% 증가한 226.5조원으로 예상
한다. DS가 증가하고 나머지는 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS는 메모리가 성장을 주도할 것으로 기대한다. 가격과 물량 모두 3분기 대비 증가할 
것으로 전망한다. Context Server에 대한 수요가 증가하고, Personal Agent의 가상메
모리 설정으로 인해 NAND 수요가 증가하면서 QLC에 대한 수요 증가 속도가 빨라질 
전망이다. 이로 인해 NAND Bit Growth는 경쟁사와 차별화된 수준일 것으로 예상한
다.  
HBM 물량도 꾸준히 증가할 것으로 기대한다. 3분기에도 HBM4를 중심으로 물량이 
크게 증가했다. HBM4는 SK하이닉스와 비슷한 물량일 것으로 예상한다. 아직 전체 
물량은 SK하이닉스가 더 많은 상황이고, HBM 내 HBM4 비중은 업계 최고 수준인 것
으로 판단한다. 26년까지는 HBM이 수익성에는 마이너스 요인이지만, 27년에는 
DRAM 수준의 영업이익이 가능할 것으로 기대한다.  
MX는 비수기 저가 라인 물량 조정으로 전체 물량은 감소하지만, 제품믹스는 개선되
어 판가는 3분기 대비 상승할 것으로 예상한다.

26년 4분기 영업이익은 138.2조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 영업이익은 26년 3분기 대비 28.7% 증가한 138.2조원으로 예
상한다. DS 사업부를 제외하고 모두 3분기 대비 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS 사업부 영업이익은 3분기 대비 30% 증가한 138.5조원으로 전망한다. DRAM, 
NAND 가격이 10% 초중반 수준으로 상승하고, 물량은 10% 중반 이상 증가하면서 3
분기 대비 영업이익률 개선을 기대하기 때문이다. HBM 물량 증가가 이익 개선에 미
치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상한다.  
디스플레이, MX는 물량 부진으로 3분기 대비 영업이익은 부진할 것으로 전망한다.

DS가 성장 주도 
DS만 보자

### Page 7

김운호  6915-5656

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
Check Point 
1. 모자란 듯한 주주환원 
26년 3분기 실적 발표에서 밝힌 주주환원 정책은 지난 8월의 내용과 다르지 않다. 3
분기 배당 30조원(주당 4,601원)을 확정한 정도이다. 잔여 배당 재원이 55~75조원으
로 구체화했고, 현금 배당과 자사주 매입을 병행하겠다고 밝혔다.  
자사주 매입 시 우선주 매입도 고려 대상이라고 언급한 것은 고무적이지만 27년부터 
새롭게 시작되는 주주환원 정책을 결정하기 위한 이사회 개최는 27년 1월에 열릴 것
으로 예고하였기에 연내 추가적인 주주환원 발표는 없을 것으로 판단한다.  
2. HBM4로 HBM 시장에서 입지 강화 
HBM4를 통해서 자신감을 회복하고 있는 것으로 판단한다. 양산 시점은 경쟁사 대비 
빨랐고, 3분기 물량은 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준인 것으로 추정한다. HBM3e가 부진했기 
때문에 전체 생산량에서 HBM에서 HBM4 비중은 가장 높은 수준일 것으로 추정한다.  
26년 전체 물량도 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준이 될 것으로 전망한다. 삼성전자는 HBM 시
장 점유율을 DRAM 수준으로 맞추겠다는 목표를 3분기에 달성한 것으로 밝혔다.  
물량이 증가하면서 가동률도 빠르게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. 아직은 DRAM과 영업
이익률에서 큰 차이가 있지만 27년에는 인상된 가격, 높아진 수율, 가동률 개선으로 
DRAM과 영업이익률 차이를 좁힐 수 있을 것으로 기대한다.  
3. QLC NAND 시장 확대 
AI 컴퓨팅 확산은 NAND에게도 성장 기회이다. AI 모델들의 Context Window가 길어
져 KV Cache 메모리에 대한 수요가 커지면서 High Endurance TLC 수요가 급증하고 
있다. DRAM을 대체할 수준의 NAND가 필요하게 되었기 때문이다.  
이 뿐만 아니라 고용량 NAND에 대한 수요도 Personal Agent의 출현으로 급속히 증
가할 것으로 기대한다. 여기에는 QLC NAND가 주로 사용된다. 메타의 Muse는 접속
자 1인에 대해서 DRAM 8GB, NAND 100GB의 가상 메모리를 설정하고 운영한다.  
Agent AI의 확산은 스토리지에 대한 수요도 급격히 확대할 것으로 기대하고, 삼성전
자에게도 기회가 될 것으로 기대한다.  
26년 4분기 NAND Bit Growth는 삼성전자가 가장 높을 것으로 예상한다.

자사주 관련 일부

### Page 8

삼성전자 (005930)

8 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 350,000원으로 하향 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 
1) 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 
2) 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, 
3) HBM 시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 
4) 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 판단하기 때문이다. 
목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황을 고려하면 적정 주가
는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사
와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 제한적일 것으로 판단하기 
때문이다.

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 
2026-10-14

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 820,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 366,500 원 
상승여력: 123.7%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 및 환율 영향으로 시장 예상치 하회 예상 
Key: 4Q26 부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 계약 수주 모멘텀 기대 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 4 행정 엔진&FDC 으로 주가 상승여력 
충분. 매수

3Q26 Preview: 컨센서스 하회하는 실적 기록 예상 
3Q26 연결 매출액 6조 464억원(+36.9% YoY, -4.5% QoQ), 영업이익 9,230억
원(+65.6% YoY, -11.2% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(1조 70억원)를 하회하는 실적 기
록할 것으로 추정한다. 이전 분기 대비 중대재해 및 하계휴가, 그리고 추석 연휴에 따른 
조업일수 감소 효과와 더불어 큰 폭의 환율 하락 영향(약 500 억원) 때문이다. 홍범도
함 화재 및 임단협 관련 일회성 비용은 이번 분기가 아닌 4Q 에 반영될 것으로 예상한
다. 해양 부문에서 트리온 FPU 프로젝트 C/O 에 따른 일부 환입 발생한 것으로 추정
되며, 해양 도크에서 컨테이너선 선체 블록 일부 건조로 고정비 감소효과 일부 상쇄 중
이다. 다만 올해 연내 혹은 내년 초 추가 해양 수주 없을 시 고정비 상승 우려 확대될 
전망이다. 동사 올해 상선 수주 목표(114.7 억불)를 이미 초과하는 수주 실적 기록한 상
황이며, 고마진 선종인 LNGC 및 VLGC/VLAC 중심으로 수주했기 때문에 ‘28~’29
년 구간 경쟁사 대비 뚜렷한 선종 믹스 효과를 기대한다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 82 만원으로 하향 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 97 만원에서 82
만원으로 하향한다. 중국의 공격적인 캐파 증설 지속과 더불어 중국 대형 조선사들의 
글로벌 선주가 발주한 LNGC 물량 및 원가 경쟁력 확대에 따른 상선 부문의 멀티플 
조정이 불가피하다는 판단이다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클
과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 FDC에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지
속되고 있는 중으로 파악된다. 올해 4 분기부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 부문의 본격적
인 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 전망이며, 해당 모멘텀에 따른 주가 상승
여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)를 유지한다.

Document 272: ANT's IPO Is Hot, But Stock Market Gets Cold Feet And Gold Panic Worsens

Source PDF: all report pdf/ANT's IPO Is Hot, But Stock Market Gets Cold Feet And Gold Panic Worsens.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-20

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: ANT's IPO Is Hot, But Stock Market Gets Cold Feet And Gold Panic Worsens
Date:    17 minutes ago
ANT's IPO Is Hot, But Stock Market Gets Cold Feet And Gold Panic Worsens

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ANT's IPO Is Hot, But Stock
Market Gets Cold Feet And Gold
Panic Worsens
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 19 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Gary Meulemans / Unsplash

I. Anthropic Makes History, Stocks Fall
Today marked Anthropic's stock market debut. It showed that enthusiasm
for AI remains strong, even as broader market sentiment has soured.
Anthropic raised $81.3 billion through its IPO, surpassing SpaceX's $75
billion offering to become the largest IPO in history. Including a concurrent

### Page 2

private placement, the deal raised $107.3 billion. The shares were priced
at $83, valuing Anthropic at $1.5 trillion and making it the world's 12th
most valuable publicly traded company. They opened at $111.50, briefly
pushing its valuation above $2 trillion, before retreating from an intraday
high of $115.10 to $95.05, still 14.5% above the IPO price.
The S&P 500 reversed an early rally this morning to fall 2.0%, while the
Nasdaq dropped 2.2% (charts). Not even the largest IPO in history could

reverse the jitters surrounding the AI trade.
The selloff extended to semiconductor stocks, with AMD plunging 7.7%
after a short-seller report raised concerns about its ties to Chinese
entities, including a US-blacklisted server manufacturer (chart). Coming
on the back of the Nvidia controversy and tighter US export controls, the

### Page 3

report provided another reminder of the political speed bumps facing the
AI trade.
For us, however, the IPO's biggest significance lies ahead. We have
argued that financial, political, and physical constraints may slow the AI
buildout, but are unlikely to halt it as long as demand for compute keeps
growing. Anthropic's future earnings reports will provide a clearer window
into AI adoption across the economy, helping us assess whether demand
for compute is strong enough to sustain one of the US economy's most

important stimulus programs: the AI buildout. We believe it is and continue
to view the recent selloff as a buying opportunity.
We are sticking with our S&P 500 target of 7,900, based on projected
2027 EPS of $425 and a forward P/E of 18.6. Industry analysts continue
to raise earnings estimates, with the consensus 2028 EPS estimate at
$496 versus our $450 forecast (chart). We suspect some irrational
exuberance, as these forecasts fail to account for the AI buildout's speed

bumps.

### Page 4

Investors, meanwhile, are increasingly skeptical of the analysts' optimistic
forecasts. So investors pushed the S&P 500 forward P/E down to 17.6
(chart). That's below last year's Liberation Day low. We think that panic
selling should abate soon.
Today's latest stock market selloff suggests that the two Bull/Bear Ratios
we track will continue to fall below their averages over time (since 1987)
this week (chart). From a contrarian perspective, that would be bullish.

### Page 5

II. US Capital Flows Supporting The Dollar
The latest Treasury International Capital (TIC) data show that foreign
demand for US financial assets remained exceptionally strong through
September. Net foreign capital inflows totaled $1.43 trillion over the 12
months through September, remaining near the record-high $1.82 trillion
last year (chart).
Equities attracted a staggering $944.7 billion, including $787.1 billion from
private foreign investors and $157.6 billion from foreign official institutions

(chart).

### Page 6

The 12-month total for foreign equity inflows has surged over the past two
years to around $1.0 trillion on a 12-month basis (chart).
Private foreign purchases of US equities reached $787.1 billion, nearly
matching the $821.5 billion pouring into US bonds (chart).

### Page 7

Interestingly, corporate bonds attracted more foreign private capital than
Treasuries, underscoring the appeal of US corporate debt (chart).
III. US Economy On A Roll
The robust foreign demand for US financial assets is consistent with the
underlying strength of the US economy. The Atlanta Fed's GDPNow
model estimates Q3 real GDP growth at a solid 3.2% (saar). Consumer
spending is expected to grow at a robust 2.5% pace, while business
investment is projected to surge 9.2% (chart).

### Page 8

The labor market remains strong too, with initial jobless claims at just
203,000 and continuing claims at 1.76 million, both near multi-year lows
(chart).
IV. Gold Keeps Falling Through Support Levels
The gold market is experiencing a selling panic. The recent selloff below
$4,000 per ounce, down to $3,682, was accompanied by record increases
in daily volume in the GLD ETF (charts). We recently pushed our $5,000
target from the end of this year to the end of next year. Our conviction

level has been greatly reduced by the selloff since the start of the Middle
East war this year. We expect that the gold price will find support around
$3,400.

### Page 9

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook
November 2, 2026
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do
About It: As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably
above $6 per gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major
theme of 2026. Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event,

on track to be one of the strongest on record. The upshot: more extreme weather
from droughts to heavy rains, warmer temperatures globally, lower crop production in
commodity-exporting economies, and higher prices across major agricultural products.
Importantly, these pressures won’t be equally distributed and will largely depend on the
magnitude and timing of weather events. Among the most vulnerable will be heavily
import-dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa and parts of Asia, while developed

economies that count food as a smaller share of consumer spending should be better
insulated. For investors, the prospect of a very strong El Niño only further reinforces our
conviction in several structural trends including a return to higher inflation and higher
interest rates, elevated commodity prices amid heightened geopolitical risk and supply
chain shortages, and greater investment in energy infrastructure and supply chain
resilience. In portfolios, we favor commodities as both an inflation hedge and diversifier.

Market View—Another Tale of Market Rotation: October offered another reminder
that headline index returns do not always tell the full story. The S&P 500 ended the month
slightly lower, but the more muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market
leadership beneath the surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the
Artificial Intelligence (AI) trade, particularly across semiconductors and memory stocks. Yet
pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S. Equities.

Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market, with Value-
oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. While it remains too early to
determine whether the shift will prove durable, October’s rotation may reflect less a retreat
from the AI theme and more a recalibration of valuations and expected returns as market
leadership broadens. For investors, the speed of the shift reinforces the importance of
diversification as the bull market enters a potentially more mature and differentiated phase.

Thought of the Week—Reorienting Around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction
to Capital Goods: AI is reshaping the physical economy, and companies are reorienting
capacity and investment plans to meet surging demand for data centers, power and
infrastructure. Industrial firms are adding or reviving capacity, while data center construction
has increased more than 60% over the past year and is outpacing other major construction
categories. At the same time, robust capital goods orders and long lead times for inputs such

as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers highlight both the strength of demand
and the supply constraints the buildout faces. Taking stock of these signposts, our view
is reinforced that the AI investment cycle remains in its middle innings, creating potential
opportunities for companies supplying the equipment and infrastructure behind the buildout.

2 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do About It
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably above $6 per
gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major theme of 2026.
Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event, on track to be one

of the strongest on record. Below we answer frequently asked questions on El Niño,
the potential impact on the global economy and markets and, most importantly, how
investors can position portfolios accordingly.
What is El Niño, and what makes this time different? El Niño is the warm phase
of the “El Niño-Southern Oscillation,” a natural climate pattern occurring every two to
seven years. Simply put, El Niño refers to the warming of sea surface temperatures in

the eastern Pacific, historically leading to drier conditions in some areas (Southeast Asia,
Australia, Central America, southern Africa) and wetter conditions in others (southern
U.S., parts of Latin America) (Exhibit 1).
What makes this episode different? Should forecasts prove correct, this El Niño is on
track to be historic. The National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration’s latest
outlook assigns at least an 83% probability that El Niño’s strength during October-

December will exceed that of any previous episode since 1950. Already, its impact has
been felt via wildfires in Indonesia, the weakest monsoon season in India since 2015,1
and severe flooding from Paraguay to Peru. The upshot: more extreme weather, hotter
temperatures globally, lower crop production in commodity-exporting economies, and
higher prices across major agricultural products.
Exhibit 1: El Niño's Impact Varies Globally.
Geography Typical rainfall pattern Specific impacts

South Asia Weaker summer monsoon risk Lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation
Southeast Asia Drier Lower rice and palm oil production, wildfire risk, food inflation
United States Wetter in the south; warmer/drier in the north Southern flood risk; lower northern heating demand
Central America & Northern South America Drier Drought, lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation, lower hydropower output
Southern Africa Drier Weaker maize harvests, food inflation, lower hydropower output

Western South America Wetter along the Pacific coast Flooding, infrastructure damage, disrupted fisheries
Southern South America Wetter Flooding and crop damage; potential yield gains in some areas
Australia Drier Drought, agricultural losses, elevated wildfire risk, food inflation
Source: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration, World Meteorological Organization, Food and Agriculture Organization, Australian Bureau of Meteorology, World Bank. Data as of

October 30, 2026.
How will El Niño impact the global economy and markets? Which regions will be
most affected? El Niño’s impact on the global economy and markets is likely to be felt
most via commodity price inflation. Alongside higher energy and fertilizer costs resulting
from war in Europe and the Middle East, El Niño could put further upward pressure on
commodities like wheat, sugar, corn, soy, cocoa and coffee as drought (or extreme rain)
weighs on crop yields and production. Latest estimates from the United Nations Food

and Agriculture Organization (FAO) suggest global food prices were up 5.8% year-over-
year (YoY) in September (Exhibit 2). Livestock is also likely to be challenged by higher
temperatures globally, while flooding has already threatened copper production in
Chile. Meanwhile, with hydropower accounting for more than half of the average annual
electricity generation in Brazil, Colombia, Peru and other Latin American countries,2
below-average rainfall in affected areas has huge implications for electricity generation.

As an example, Colombia’s natural gas demand nearly doubled during the 2023-2024 El
Niño event.2 Another key watchpoint will be whether drier conditions reduce water levels
1 India Meteorological Department, Bloomberg. Refers to rainfall between June and September as of September 30, 2026.
2 International Energy Agency, as of October 7, 2026
Investment Implications
We expect El Niño to place upward
pressure on agricultural goods
prices globally. We continue to

see commodities as a strategic
diversifier and hedge against inflation
in portfolios. The U.S. remains our
preferred region with companies in
Industrials and Materials as potential
beneficiaries. Meanwhile, economic
impacts to the emerging world will vary
by country but the broader Emerging
Market (EM) equity market could be
less exposed given its significant tilt
toward Technology.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
in the Panama Canal and other key shipping routes, particularly in light of the Strait of
Hormuz closure.
These pressures won’t be equally distributed and will heavily depend on this El Niño’s
severity. Among the more vulnerable to higher agricultural prices will be heavily import-
dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa, and parts of Asia. So too will economies
that count food as a larger share of consumer spending such as the Philippines (36%),

Indonesia (33%), Egypt (33%), India (28%), and Vietnam (26%) (Exhibit 3). Developed
economies like the U.S. (6%), Canada (10%), and Germany (11%) stand to be better
insulated from a food-related shock to headline inflation.
Within the U.S., some impacts could be positive with a potentially milder winter reducing
heating costs and wetter conditions in the South improving agricultural conditions. A recent
paper from the Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco found that, in addition to putting

upward pressure on personal consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation, strong El Niño
episodes have been historically associated with a temporary boost to employment in the
following year via better construction conditions in the North and weather-event recovery
efforts.3 In California and parts of the Southwest, El Niño will be yet another stress test for
property insurers as flooding and landslide risks increase.
For more vulnerable developing economies, meanwhile, higher food and energy costs could

present a policy dilemma. On one hand, governments in vulnerable areas may move to
introduce fiscal relief to support households and boost growth. History points to plenty of
examples of this, such as Indonesia’s cash assistance in late 2023 or Thailand’s $1.8 billion
in loans to farmers in 2015. On the other hand, elevated inflation expectations and, in
some cases, weaker currencies could limit EM central banks’ room to ease monetary policy,
particularly with several major developed central banks aligned in hiking interest rates. Also

complicating matters: Global sovereign debt surpassed $110 trillion in Q2 2026, leaving
less fiscal space to absorb incoming shocks.4
How can investors position portfolios accordingly? In many ways, the prospect of a
very strong El Niño only reinforces structural trends we’ve identified as defining the coming
years: a return to higher inflation and higher interest rates; elevated commodity prices
amid heightened geopolitical risk and resource/supply shortages; and greater investment in

energy infrastructure and supply chain resilience.
Considering these themes, we continue to like commodities not just as a hedge against
higher inflation but also as a diversification tool in portfolios given their negative correlation
with Equities and bonds. That’s not to mention secular forces placing upward pressure on
commodity prices such as the resource-intensive AI buildout and broader protectionism. To
wit, the Bloomberg Commodity Index, which spans oil, gold, various metals and agricultural

goods has gained nearly 30% this year, handily outperforming the S&P 500 (+1%) and the
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index (-1%).
Within Equities, the U.S. is still our preferred region. While upside risk to inflation remains
a risk to watch going into 2027, food is a relatively small share of the consumer basket in
the U.S., and neither strong services spending nor capital investment should be materially
affected by El Niño. Companies within the Industrials and Materials sectors focused on

precision agriculture, water management and crop protection may be positioned to benefit.
Import-dependent EM economies remain particularly exposed, though impacts will vary
widely depending on the magnitude and timing of weather events. It’s worth noting too that
EM Equities remain heavily concentrated in East Asian markets with substantial exposure to
technology, leaving the broader index arguably less exposed to the economic ramifications
of El Niño than in past episodes. Indeed, even after recent weakness in semiconductor

names, Taiwan, Korea, and China together account for a near-70% of the broader MSCI
Emerging Markets Index.5 We maintain a slight overweight to the region in portfolios given
earnings strength but favor an active approach.

4 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Another Tale of Market Rotation
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
October offered another reminder that headline index returns do not always tell the
full story. The S&P 500 ended the month slightly lower, down almost 1%, but the more
muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market leadership beneath the
surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the AI trade, particularly

across semiconductors and memory stocks, with the Philadelphia Semiconductor Index
experiencing a 19% intramonth drawdown. The dominant concerns in recent months
have centered on the sustainability of AI infrastructure spending and whether the scale
of investment will ultimately generate sufficient returns. Those only intensified amid a
sharp rise in bond yields after the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield spiked above 5.3% as of
October 5 for the first time since 2002, raising scrutiny over AI financing conditions and

whether higher borrowing costs could increase hurdle rates for continued investment
and slow the pace of capital spending.
Yet the pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S.
Equities. Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market,
with Value-oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. Value defeated
Growth in October, with the Russell 1000 Value Index outperforming its Growth

counterpart by 10 percentage points, an outcome seen in only about 1% of months since
1979. The move extends Value's outperformance for the year, with the index up 23%
year-to-date compared with down almost 1% for Growth. Participation also improved.
The equal-weighted S&P 500 outperformed its market-cap-weighted counterpart by its
widest monthly gap since 2009 (Exhibit 4A), while the share of S&P 500 constituents
trading above their 200-day moving average rebounded to 62% from just 43% at the

end of September, and 76% of stocks outperformed the index for the month.
Exhibit 4: Market Leadership Has Been Shifting Beneath the Surface.
A) October Saw a Sharp Broadening in Market Leadership. B) Value Has Quietly Led Growth in 2026.

not possible to invest directly in an index.

The latest rotation is not the first attempt at a change in market leadership this year.
The more important question is what that shift represents: a fundamental reassessment
of the AI trade or another step toward broader, more mature market leadership?
Recent Q3 Technology earnings results have sharpened that debate. Investors are
increasingly looking beyond the scale of AI spending to companies’ ability to translate
that investment into durable growth and attractive returns. The result may be greater

dispersion not only between Growth and Value, but also among AI leaders as investors
become more discerning about which companies can justify the scale of their
investments.
Investment Implications
We continue to look for potential
missing opportunities and favor
diversification across and within asset
classes in a multi-asset portfolio.
In particular, we see potential
opportunities to broaden exposure
beyond AI leaders toward areas
where earnings, capital spending and

productivity trends are improving,
including Value, Small- and Mid-caps,
and select non-U.S. markets, particularly
EMs and Japan. As market leadership
evolves, maintaining diversified
exposures and periodically rebalancing
portfolios can help investors participate
in a broader set of possible return
drivers without becoming overly
dependent on any single theme.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
But greater selectivity does not necessarily signal the end of the AI investment cycle.
YoY growth rates may become harder to maintain after two years of rapid acceleration
as the comparison base rises, but absolute levels of AI capital spending remain elevated.
What matters more is whether spending translates into strong productivity and earnings
growth ahead and whether AI demand continues to support investment. October’s

volatility may therefore reflect less a retreat from the AI theme and more a recalibration
of valuations and expected returns, as other areas of the market begin to contribute
more meaningfully to market performance.
Several developments ahead will help determine whether that rotation has staying
power. One important test is whether broader participation can persist if pressure on AI
stocks continues. Continued strength outside the AI trade would support the case that

investors are reallocating capital rather than broadly reducing risk. The path of monetary
policy will also matter as investors assess where borrowing costs may be headed. The
Federal Reserve’s not-so-hawkish hold in October offered some reassurance, but the
path ahead remains dependent on the evolving balance between inflation and growth.
Any signs of resolution of geopolitical tensions remain an important swing factor
particularly as recent flashpoints in the Middle East have pressured energy prices higher

again. Results of the midterm elections should also provide greater visibility into the
policy landscape for the next few years. While the market does not need every clearing
event to resolve favorably, greater clarity around these uncertainties could help narrow
the range of potential outcomes facing investors.
For investors, October’s sudden change in leadership reinforces the importance of
diversification. Leadership can shift quickly, leaving portfolios that become overly

dependent on a single theme, sector or style particularly vulnerable when it does. We
remain constructive on U.S. Equities and believe that the market may be transitioning
more toward a mid-cycle phase in which returns become broader but also more
measured compared to the sharp acceleration earlier in the cycle. That argues not for
abandoning the market’s long-term winners, but for complementing them with exposure
to additional sources of returns across style, size, sector and region.

### Page 6

6 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Reorienting around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction to Capital Goods
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
For all the questions (and recent market weakness) hanging over the AI trade, from
monetization and cash burn to financing risk and overcapacity, a measurable aspect is
the buildout itself. Data center construction is lifting construction spending and capital

goods orders, while companies are adding and reviving capacity to meet AI-related
demand. Still, planning delays, access to power, labor scarcity, local politics and the cost
of capital remain meaningful constraints on the buildout’s pace or profitability.
Corporate Plans. Take Caterpillar as one example that has resumed production of a
10-megawatt natural gas turbine discontinued back in 2022. A 175-year old glassmaker,
Corning, is leaning into optical connectivity as AI clusters require faster connections of

optical fiber cabling between servers. Caterpillar and Corning are just two companies
among others pivoting to supply data centers.
Construction to Capital Goods. The AI reorientation is evident in construction
spending as well, where data center construction has outpaced every other major
residential and nonresidential category. Over the last year AI-related construction on data
centers has surged more than 60% while spending on residential, lodging, commercial

or office has declined notably (Exhibit 5A). Data center construction is running at an $85
billion (B) annualized rate this year, versus roughly $49B a year earlier, and now accounts
for 11% of private nonresidential construction, up from just 2% in 2022 according to
the Census Bureau. Similarly, demand for artificial intelligence business equipment and
transportation equipment drove an increase in durable goods orders in September on the
back of new orders for U.S.-manufactured capital goods, suggesting business spending

on equipment remained robust in Q3. Another factor, see Exhibit 5B, where critical
components such as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers continue to
face long lead times that affect delivery timelines and ultimate profitability for new data
center development.
These signposts reinforce our view that we are only in the middle innings of the AI
investment cycle. From an investment perspective, huge demand for data centers, power
systems, networking infrastructure, specialized chips and other equipment continues

to strain supply, creating both bottlenecks and potential opportunities for companies
powering the buildout.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500/Market-cap Weighted Index is a market-capitalization-weighted index that is widely regarded as the best single gauge of large-cap U.S. equities. The index includes 500 leading

companies and covers approximately 80% of available market capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index includes the same constituents as the capitalization weighted S&P 500, but each company in the S&P 500 EWI is allocated a fixed weight - or 0.2% of the
index total at each quarterly rebalance.
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index is a broad-based, market-capitalization-weighted benchmark that measures the performance of the U.S. investment-grade, U.S. dollar-denominated,

fixed-rate taxable bond market
Bloomberg Commodity Index is a broadly diversified financial benchmark that tracks the price movements of physical commodities through futures contracts.
MSCI Emerging Market Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets (EM) countries.
United Nations Food and Agriculture Food Price Index is a measure of the monthly change in international prices of a basket of food commodities.
Philadelphia Semiconductor Index is a market-cap-weighted index focusing on 30 leading semiconductor firms, offering insights into the tech market.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

Document 298: Central banks_ a pause while waiting for more information (2026-10-29)

Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 2

### Page 1

Paris, 30 October 2026

EcoFlash

Central banks: a pause while waiting for more information

As expected, the Fed, the ECB, and the BoJ left their rates unchanged this week. This pause, however, does not mark 
the end of the cycle. The ECB’s tone is becoming more balanced, weighing inflationary risks against tightening financial 
conditions, but one final rate hike in December remains our base case. Across the Atlantic, Kevin Warsh describes an 
environment conducive to further tightening, while remaining cautious about the risk of a credit shock. In Japan, the 
lasting exit from the deflationary cycle is taking shape, and monetary policy adjustment is expected to continue 
gradually. We anticipate two rate hikes by the Fed by January and four by the BoJ by the second half of 2028.

Eurozone: A more balanced tone, but the course remains unchanged. The ECB’s statement marks a shift from September. 
It now explicitly acknowledges both upside risks to inflation (the magnitude and persistence of the energy shock) and downside 
pressures (tightening financial conditions). The statement that inflation would remain “well above target for an extended period” 
no longer appears. In essence, however, there is little change. A rate hike in December remains our base case scenario, widely 
anticipated and implicitly signaled by Christine Lagarde during the Q&A (“If the current situation is not called into question by the 
next set of economic data, the path is clear”). Lagarde presented the rise in sovereign yields as a global phenomenon that is 
tightening financial conditions; regarding the TPI, she stuck to the usual stance: the ECB will intervene if monetary policy 
transmission were to be hampered “by disorderly movements not justified by fundamentals”. By December, the indirect and 
second-round effects of the energy shock will be closely scrutinised. From our perspective, September’s producer prices offer 
clearer signals, but the increase remains limited to a few sectors: energy, electricity-intensive industries and electrical equipment, 
driven by IA1. For now, companies in the Eurozone, particularly in the services sector, appear to be absorbing the shock. The 
European Commission’s survey released this Thursday indicates a rise in the sales price index in October, which remains in line 
with recent months.

United States: The Fed navigates between risks. The FOMC pauses after September’s rate hike and maintains the federal 
funds target at 3.75%-4.0%. However, Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh outlined a landscape conducive to further tightening: stronger 
growth, “rather positive” labor market conditions, and, above all, inflation that is “too high.” As a result, two dissenting votes (out 
of 12) were cast in favor of a 25bps hike. But the committee also appears to fear the adverse consequences of tightening too 
quickly, which is why it is waiting to “accumulate more information.” AI is increasingly at the heart of monetary policymaking. In 
particular, K. Warsh warned against the risks of an abrupt tightening of interest rate conditions, including for the AI investment 
cycle - which triggered a steepening of the yield curve. He also announced that he would soon (on November 16) attend a hearing 
of the “Productivity and Employment” task force to “refine his views” on the implications of AI for the dual mandate. Governor Lisa 
Cook, who voted for a 25bps hike, had noted ahead of the committee meeting “ongoing inflationary pressures” and a risk that they 
might not “subside quickly”. In addition, K. Warsh announced that there would be fewer meetings resulting in rate decisions: 7 in 
2027 and 6 in 2028, compared with 8 today. The change is expected to take effect starting in the fall of 2027. The meetings freed 
up by this change would allow the committee to focus on “strategic deliberations” and long-term issues.

Japan: A status quo, but a tightening cycle set to continue as the economy emerges from deflation. The Bank of Japan 
decided, in line with our forecasts and the consensus, to keep its policy rate target steady at 1.25% at its October 29-30 meeting. 
One dissenting vote called for a rate hike (25bps), contrasting with the two dovish dissenting votes (opposed to a rate hike) at the 
previous meeting. As in the US, the decision is justified by the need to further analyse risks to economic activity, prices, and 
financial conditions. Above all, the central bank’s outlook for economic activity and prices marks a turning point: it explicitly states 
that “core inflation has reached a level generally in line with price stability” and that it is “expected to remain there.” Governor Ueda 
refined this by noting that it is now necessary to “anchor” inflation at this level before declaring the target achieved. Furthermore, 
the emphasis on “upside risks to prices” and the fact that any rise in core inflation is now deemed “undesirable” indicate that 
monetary tightening will continue.

The monetary tightening cycle continues. The next steps are likely to be more significant in the United States, where inflation 
has exceeded the target for nearly six years and coexists with a favorable outlook for growth and employment. In the Eurozone, 
the rise in inflation is primarily due to a supply shock in energy costs (though it is also fueled by demand in the United States). It 
continues to have little impact on measures of core inflation, including those tracked by the ECB, such as the HICP; and 
households’ long-term inflation expectations remain anchored. The degree of monetary tightening also differs. In real terms, the 
ECB’s current rates (the deposit facility rate deflated by the core CPI) hover around 0%, a level that more or less corresponds to 
estimates of the natural rate. The Fed, meanwhile, remains in accommodative territory: the real rate (Fed Funds deflated by Core

1 This is a point underscored by the first PPI releases for September (Spain, Italy).

### Page 2

PCE) is at around 0.8%, 40bps below the committee’s implied long-term level (1.2%). Japan remains an outlier: the rise in rates 
preceded the inflationary shock and, until now, has been more a matter of normalization. It is therefore more extensive and spread 
out over time. The central bank is adjusting to the new inflation regime and remains cautious in its exit from accommodative 
policies. The starting point is significantly lower, with real rates still negative, but the adjustment cycle should ultimately bring it to 
the upper end of the neutral range estimated by the BoJ (1.1%-2.5%). We therefore anticipate one final rate hike by the ECB in 
December, two more hikes by the Fed, first in December and then in January, and four spaced-out hikes by the BoJ through the 
second half of 2028.

Weekly Economics
U.S. Week Ahead
Existing Home Sales • Thursday
Housing market conditions remain highly unfavorable. Mortgage
rates are the primary headwind, and they have climbed even higher
alongside the rise in longer-term yields. At the same time, low
supply is preventing a broad correction in home prices, leaving
prospective buyers squeezed on two fronts.
We expect that existing home sales slid 3.4% in August to an
annualized rate of 3.92 million. Behind this forecast is ongoing

weakness in mortgage applications and a recent bounce in
mortgage rates, which rose from 6.43% at the beginning of July to
6.66% by month-end.
We do not expect affordability conditions to meaningfully improve
in the near term. Supply is likely to remain constrained while high
mortgage rates discourage mobility. And mortgage rates are apt
to remain elevated amid firm economic growth expectations and
heavy government and corporate debt issuance.

Economics Week Ahead Economics
CPI • Friday
Headline inflation looks to have picked up last month. We estimate
the CPI rose 0.40% in August, with gasoline prices increasing a little
over 4% as renewed tensions in the Middle East pushed oil higher.
Grocery prices also likely rebounded after modestly declining in July.
Outside of food and energy, price pressures appear little changed.
We expect the core CPI to rise 0.23%, essentially matching July's

pace. Core goods inflation should remain firm, led by another solid
increase in used vehicle prices as consumer prices continue to
catch up to the trend indicated at wholesale auctions. Meantime,
household furnishings and other tariff-sensitive categories appear
to be stabilizing after declining earlier this year.
Core services inflation should be little changed with another 0.2%
monthly increase. But underneath the surface, we expect a modest
pickup in travel (led by lodging away from home) to offset slightly

cooler medical care and housing cost growth.
All told, we expect the report to show that while headline inflation
remains influenced by the ebbs and flows of the conflict in the
Middle East, core inflation remains contained. Should our forecast
prove correct, core CPI will ease to 2.4% year-over-year, in line with
its 6-month annualized rate but above the more recent 3-month
annualized pace of 1.7%.

Weekly Economics
G10 Week Ahead
European Central Bank Monetary Policy Meeting •
Thursday
We expect the European Central Bank (ECB) to raise the Deposit
Rate by 25 bps to 2.50% next week. August headline inflation
remained elevated at 3.3% year-over-year, though softer core
and services inflation suggest underlying pressures continue to
moderate. Still, the more hawkish tone of the July minutes and
renewed rise in energy prices should keep policymakers cautious.

The recent escalation in the Middle East has pushed fuel prices
at the pump close to their 2022 peaks, while natural gas storage
remains below historical levels, leaving the Eurozone vulnerable
to further energy price increases. The ECB is unlikely to respond
aggressively if the shock remains confined to energy given the
downside risks to growth. However, a broader rise in food inflation
linked to El Niño would be more concerning, as highly visible prices

could lift inflation expectations and generate second-round effects.
For now, resilient Q3 activity, elevated headline inflation and
contained wage growth support one final hike. The ECB will likely
retain and emphasize its scenario-based outlook in the updated
staff projections given continued uncertainty around the conflict
and energy prices. The policy path beyond September will depend
on whether energy and food pressures persist and begin to spread,

which could leave room for further tightening.

U.K. Monthly GDP • Friday
We expect UK GDP to rise 0.2% month-over-month in July, with
growth of 0.3% on a three-month-over-three-month basis.

Activity likely moderated after June’s strong rebound, though
services remained supportive as the July services PMI rose to 52.1.
Retail sales volumes fell 0.5% month-over-month, while softer
manufacturing activity likely reflected some reversal of earlier
precautionary stockbuilding.
The moderation appears more consistent with payback from
June than a broader deterioration in momentum. August PMIs
remain consistent with Q3 GDP growth of around 0.3% quarter-

over-quarter. Still, the renewed escalation in the Middle East and
associated rise in gas and energy prices adds upside risk to inflation
and could weigh on household demand. Against this backdrop and
with Bank of England hawks continuing to emphasize the need for
tighter policy, we expect the BoE to remain cautious and continue
to look for a rate hike in Q4. -1.0%
